256: diff --git a/docs/install.md b/docs/install.md
257: index 067163a..bd3232b 100644
258: --- a/docs/install.md
259: +++ b/docs/install.md
260: @@ -486,19 +486,31 @@ groups
261:  Additionally, to prevent unauthorized users from gaining root-level privileges through Containerlab, the usage of privileged Containerlab commands is gated behind a Unix user group membership check. Privileged Containerlab commands can only be performed by users who are part of the `clab_admins` group.  
262:  By default (starting with version `0.63.0`), the `clab_admins` Unix group is created during the initial installation of Containerlab, and the user installing Containerlab is automatically added to this user group. Additional users who require access to privileged Containerlab commands should also be added to this user group.
263:  
264:  Users who are _not_ part of this group can still execute non-privileged commands, such as:
265:  
266: +* completion
267:  * exec (requires `docker` group membership)
268:  * generate
269:  * graph
270:  * inspect (requires `docker` group membership)
271:  * save
272: +* tools cert
273: +* validate
274:  * version (no upgrade)
275:  
276:  Non-privileged commands are executed as the user running the Containerlab commands. Privileged commands are executed as root during runtime.  
277: -Non-privileged command execution is only supported when the default container runtime, Docker.
278: +All of these run without root privileges only with the default container runtime, Docker. With other runtimes, such as Podman, only the commands that don't use the container runtime do:
279: +
280: +* completion
281: +* generate (no `--deploy`)
282: +* graph with `--dot`, `--mermaid` or `--offline` (no `--drawio`, not with `--name` alone)
283: +* help
284: +* tools cert
285: +* tui with `--demo`
286: +* validate (not with `--name` alone)
287: +* version (no upgrade)
288:  
289:  To allow _any user on the host_ to use all Containerlab commands, simply delete the `clab_admins` Unix group.
290:  
291:  /// danger
292:  Much like the `docker` group, any users part of the `clab_admins` group are effectively given root-level privileges to the system running Containerlab.

(End of file - total 292 lines)

<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Think Before Coding\n13: \n14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**\n15: \n16: Before implementing:\n17: \n18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.\n19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.\n20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.\n21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.\n22: \n23: ## 2. Simplicity First\n24: \n25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**\n26: \n27: - No features beyond what was asked.\n28: - No abstractions for single-use code.\n29: - No \"flexibility\" or \"configurability\" that wasn't requested.\n30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.\n31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.\n32: \n33: Ask yourself: \"Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?\" If yes, simplify.\n34: \n35: ## 3. Surgical Changes\n36: \n37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**\n38: \n39: When editing existing code:\n40: \n41: - Don't \"improve\" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.\n42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.\n43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.\n44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.\n45: \n46: When your changes create orphans:\n47: \n48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.\n49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.\n50: \n51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.\n52: \n53: ## 4. Loop until verified.**\n56: \n57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:\n58: \n59: - \"Add validation\" → \"Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass\"\n60: - \"Fix the bug\" → \"Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass\"\n61: - \"Refactor X\" → \"Ensure tests pass before and after\"\n62: \n63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:\n64: \n65: ```\n66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]\n67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]\n68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]\n69: ```\n70: \n71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria (\"make it work\") require constant clarification.\n72: \n73: ---\n74: \n75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.\n\n(End of file - total 75 lines)\n</content>",

        "text": "---\ndescription: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Karpathy behavioral guidelines\n\nBehavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.\n\n**Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.\n\n## 1. Think Before Coding\n\n**Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**\n\nBefore implementing:\n\n- State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.\n- If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.\n- If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.\n- If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.\n\n## 2. Simplicity First\n\n**Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**\n\n- No features beyond what was asked.\n- No abstractions for single-use code.\n- No \"flexibility\" or \"configurability\" that wasn't requested.\n- No error handling for impossible scenarios.\n- If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.\n\nAsk yourself: \"Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?\" If yes, simplify.\n\n## 3. Surgical Changes\n\n**Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**\n\nWhen editing existing code:\n\n- Don't \"improve\" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.\n- Don't refactor things that aren't broken.\n- Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.\n- If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.\n\nWhen your changes create orphans:\n\n- Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.\n- Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.\n\nThe test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.\n\n## 4. Goal-Driven Execution\n\n**Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**\n\nTransform tasks into verifiable goals:\n\n- \"Add validation\" → \"Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass\"\n- \"Fix the bug\" → \"Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass\"\n- \"Refactor X\" → \"Ensure tests pass before and after\"\n\nFor multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:\n\n```\n1. [Step] → verify: [check]\n2. [Step] → verify: [check]\n3. [Step] → verify: [check]\n```\n\nStrong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria (\"make it work\") require constant clarification.\n\n---\n\n**These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.",

<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

49: // Entrypoint returns the root cobra command to be executed or errors if it cannot do so.
50: func Entrypoint() (*cobra.Command, error) {
51: 	o := GetOptions()
52: 
53: 	c := &cobra.Command{
54: 		Use:   "containerlab",
55: 		Short: "deploy container based lab environments with a user-defined interconnections",
56: 		PersistentPreRunE: func(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
57: 			return preRunFn(cobraCmd, o)
58: 		},
59: 		Aliases:      []string{"clab"},
60: 		SilenceUsage: true,
61: 	}
62: 
63: 	c.PersistentFlags().CountVarP(
64: 		&o.Global.DebugCount,
65: 		"debug",
66: 		"d",
67: 		"enable debug mode",
68: 	)
69: 	c.PersistentFlags().StringVarP(
70: 		&o.Global.TopologyFile,

73: 		o.Global.TopologyFile,
74: 		"path to the topology definition file, a directory containing one, 'stdin', or a URL",

78: 		"vars",
79: 		"",

82: 	)

85: 		"name",
86: 		"",

94: 	)

101: 	)

104: 		"runtime",
105: 		"r",
106: 		o.Global.Runtime,

108: 	)

110: 		&o.Global.LogLevel,
111: 		"log-level",
112: 		"",
113: 		o.Global.LogLevel,
114: 		"logging level; one of [trace, debug, info, warning, error, fatal]",
115: 	)
116: 
117: 	err := c.MarkPersistentFlagFilename("topo", "*.yaml", "*.yml")
118: 	if err != nil {
119: 		return nil, err
120: 	}
121: 
122: 	for _, f := range subcommandRegisterFuncs() {
123: 		cmd, err := f(o)
124: 		if err != nil {
125: 			return nil, err
126: 		}

129: 	}
130:

132: 	if err := initViper(c); err != nil {
133: 		return nil, err
134: 	}
135: 
136: 	return c, nil
137: }
138: 
139: func preRunFn(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, o *Options) error {
140: 	// Update options from viper (environment variables take precedence over defaults)
141: 	if v != nil {
142: 		updateOptionsFromViper(cobraCmd, o)
143: 	}
144: 	applyLabRuntimeDefaultTimeout(cobraCmd, o)
145: 
146: 	// setting log level
147: 	switch {
148: 	case o.Global.DebugCount > 0:
149: 		log.SetLevel(log.DebugLevel)
150: 	default:
151: 		l, err := log.ParseLevel(o.Global.LogLevel)
152: 		if err != nil {
153: 			return err
154: 		}
155: 
156: 		log.SetLevel(l)
157: 	}
158: 
159: 	// initializes the version manager that goes off and fetches current version in
160: 	// the background for us
161: 	initVersionManager(cobraCmd.Context())
162: 
163: 	// setting output to stderr, so that json outputs can be parsed
164: 	log.SetOutput(os.Stderr)
165: 
166: 	log.SetTimeFormat(time.TimeOnly)
167: 
168: 	if err := checkLabRuntimeCommandSupport(cobraCmd, o.Global.Runtime); err != nil {
169: 		return err
170: 	}
171: 
172: 	err := clabutils.DropRootPrivs()
173: 	if err != nil {
174: 		return err
175: 	}
176: 	if globalRuntimeRequiresRoot(o.Global.Runtime) && !commandIsRuntimeIndependent(cobraCmd, o) {
177: 		err := clabutils.CheckAndGetRootPrivs()
178: 		if err != nil {
179: 			return err
180: 		}
181: 	}
182: 
183: 	return getTopoFilePath(cobraCmd, o)
184: }
185: 
186: // applyLabRuntimeDefaultTimeout gives remote, controller-driven lab runtimes enough time to load
187: // large node images. An explicit flag or environment value always wins. Local Docker and Podman
188: // retain their existing two-minute default.
189: func applyLabRuntimeDefaultTimeout(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, o *Options) {
190: 	if !clablabruntime.IsLabRuntimeName(o.Global.Runtime) {
191: 		return
192: 	}
193: 
194: 	timeoutFlag := cobraCmd.Flag("timeout")
195: 	if timeoutFlag != nil && timeoutFlag.Changed {
196: 		return
197: 	}
198: 
199: 	if value, ok := os.LookupEnv(envPrefix + "_TIMEOUT"); ok && strings.TrimSpace(value) != "" {
200: 		return
201: 	}
202: 
203: 	o.Global.Timeout = defaultLabRuntimeTimeout
204: }
205: 
206: func globalRuntimeRequiresRoot(name string) bool {
207: 	return name != "" &&
208: 		name != clabruntimedocker.RuntimeName &&
209: 		!commandSkipsRoot(name)
210: }
211: 
212: func commandSkipsRoot(name string) bool {
213: 	return clablabruntime.IsLabRuntimeName(name)
214: }
215: 
216: // runtimeIndependentCommands don't use the container runtime, so they don't need root
217: // privileges with any runtime. Commands in this list that can still need root, such as
218: // generate --deploy, check for it themselves.
219: var runtimeIndependentCommands = map[string]struct{}{ //nolint:gochecknoglobals
220: 	"help":                          {},
221: 	cobra.ShellCompRequestCmd:       {},
222: 	cobra.ShellCompNoDescRequestCmd: {},
223: 	"version":                       {},
224: 	"version.check":                 {},
225: 	"completion":                    {},
226: 	"generate":                      {},
227: 	"tools.cert.ca.create":          {},
228: 	"tools.cert.sign":               {},
229: }
230: 
231: // commandIsRuntimeIndependent reports whether a command runs without the container runtime,
232: // so that it doesn't need root privileges. Some commands only do so with certain flags.
233: func commandIsRuntimeIndependent(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, o *Options) bool {
234: 	commandPath := getCommandPath(cobraCmd)
235: 	if _, ok := runtimeIndependentCommands[commandPath]; ok {
236: 		return true
237: 	}
238: 
239: 	switch commandPath {
240: 	case "graph":
241: 		// drawio runs the clab-io-draw container through Docker
242: 		return topologyFromFile(o) && !o.Graph.GenerateDrawIO &&
243: 			(o.Graph.GenerateDotFile || o.Graph.GenerateMermaid || o.Graph.Offline)
244: 	case "validate":
245: 		return topologyFromFile(o)
246: 	case "tui":
247: 		return o.TUI.Demo
248: 	}
249: 
250: 	return false
251: }
252: 
253: // topologyFromFile reports whether the topology is read from a file. A lab given by its name
254: // alone is read from its containers.
255: func topologyFromFile(o *Options) bool {
256: 	return o.Global.TopologyFile != "" || o.Global.TopologyName == ""
257: }
258: 
259: // labRuntimeUnsupportedCommands operate on local containers or host networking
260: // and have no lab runtime equivalent.
261: var labRuntimeUnsupportedCommands = map[string]struct{}{
262: 	"tools": {},
263: }
264: 
265: // labRuntimeSupportedCommands are the commands of an unsupported group that lab runtimes do
266: // support, by command path.
267: var labRuntimeSupportedCommands = map[string]struct{}{ //nolint:gochecknoglobals
268: 	"tools.netem": {},
269: }
270: 
271: // graphTopologyFileFlags select graph modes that render from the topology file alone, without
272: // running containers. Lab runtimes support only these modes.
273: var graphTopologyFileFlags = []string{ //nolint:gochecknoglobals
274: 	"dot",
275: 	"mermaid",
276: 	"drawio",
277: 	"offline",
278: }
279: 
280: // labRuntimeUnsupportedFlags is the explicit CLI compatibility contract for controller-driven
281: // runtimes. A flag listed here must fail before topology parsing or remote mutation instead of
282: // being accepted and silently ignored by the adapter.
283: var labRuntimeUnsupportedFlags = map[string][]string{ //nolint:gochecknoglobals
284: 	"deploy": {
285: 		"graph", "ipv4-subnet", "ipv6-subnet", "network", "node-filter",
286: 		"restore", "restore-all", "skip-labdir-acl", "skip-post-deploy", "export-template",
287: 	},
288: 	"destroy": {
289: 		"graceful", "keep-mgmt-net", "node-filter",
290: 	},
291: 	"graph": {
292: 		"node-filter",
293: 	},
294: 	"redeploy": {
295: 		"graceful", "graph", "ipv4-subnet", "ipv6-subnet", "keep-mgmt-net",
296: 		"network", "skip-labdir-acl", "skip-post-deploy", "export-template",
297: 	},
298: }
299: 
300: func checkLabRuntimeCommandSupport(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, runtimeName string) error {
301: 	if !clablabruntime.IsLabRuntimeName(runtimeName) {
302: 		return nil
303: 	}
304: 
305: 	for cmd := cobraCmd; cmd != nil; cmd = cmd.Parent() {
306: 		if _, ok := labRuntimeSupportedCommands[getCommandPath(cmd)]; ok {
307: 			break
308: 		}
309: 		if _, ok := labRuntimeUnsupportedCommands[cmd.Name()]; ok {
310: 			return fmt.Errorf("the %q command is not supported with lab runtime %q",
311: 				cmd.Name(), runtimeName)
312: 		}
313: 	}
314: 
315: 	if getCommandPath(cobraCmd) == "graph" && !graphRendersFromTopologyFile(cobraCmd) {
316: 		return fmt.Errorf(
317: 			"the %q command needs running containers with lab runtime %q unless it renders "+
318: 				"from the topology file alone; use --offline, --dot, --mermaid or --drawio",
319: 			"graph", runtimeName)
320: 	}
321: 
322: 	var unsupported []string
323: 	for _, flagName := range labRuntimeUnsupportedFlags[getCommandPath(cobraCmd)] {
324: 		if labRuntimeFlagWasSet(cobraCmd, flagName) {
325: 			unsupported = append(unsupported, "--"+flagName)
326: 		}
327: 	}
328: 	if len(unsupported) != 0 {
329: 		sort.Strings(unsupported)
330: 
331: 		return fmt.Errorf(
332: 			"flag(s) %s are not supported with the %q command and lab runtime %q",
333: 			strings.Join(unsupported, ", "),
334: 			cobraCmd.Name(),
335: 			runtimeName,
336: 		)
337: 	}
338: 
339: 	return nil
340: }
341: 
342: func graphRendersFromTopologyFile(cobraCmd *cobra.Command) bool {
343: 	for _, flagName := range graphTopologyFileFlags {
344: 		flag := cobraCmd.Flag(flagName)
345: 		if flag != nil && flag.Value.String() == "true" &&
346: 			labRuntimeFlagWasSet(cobraCmd, flagName) {
347: 			return true
348: 		}
349: 	}
350: 
351: 	return false
352: }
353: 
354: func labRuntimeFlagWasSet(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, flagName string) bool {
355: 	flag := cobraCmd.Flag(flagName)
356: 	if flag == nil {
357: 		return false
358: 	}
359: 
360: 	if flag.Changed {
361: 		return true
362: 	}
363: 
364: 	if v == nil {
365: 		return false
366: 	}
367: 
368: 	commandKey := getCommandPath(cobraCmd) + "." + flagName
369: 	if v.IsSet(commandKey) {
370: 		return true
371: 	}
372: 
373: 	return v.IsSet(flagName)
374: }
375: 
376: // getTopoFilePath finds *.clab.y*ml file in the current working directory
377: // if the file was not specified.
378: // If the topology file refers to a git repository, it will be cloned to the current directory.
379: // Errors if more than one file is found by the glob path.
380: func getTopoFilePath(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, o *Options) error { // skipcq: GO-R1005
381: 	// set commands which may use topo file find functionality, the rest don't need it
382: 	if cobraCmd.Name() != "deploy" &&
383: 		cobraCmd.Name() != "destroy" &&
384: 		cobraCmd.Name() != "start" &&
385: 		cobraCmd.Name() != "stop" &&
386: 		cobraCmd.Name() != "restart" &&
387: 		cobraCmd.Name() != "redeploy" &&
388: 		cobraCmd.Name() != "inspect" &&
389: 		cobraCmd.Name() != "save" &&
390: 		cobraCmd.Name() != "graph" &&
391: 		cobraCmd.Name() != "validate" &&
392: 		cobraCmd.Name() != "logs" &&
393: 		cobraCmd.Name() != "capture" &&
394: 		cobraCmd.Name() != "interfaces" {
395: 		return nil
396: 	}
397: 
398: 	// start/stop/restart topology lookup applies only to top-level lifecycle commands.
399: 	// nested commands like tools api-server start|stop are excluded.
400: 	if (cobraCmd.Name() == "start" || cobraCmd.Name() == "stop" || cobraCmd.Name() == "restart") &&
401: 		(cobraCmd.Parent() == nil || cobraCmd.Parent().Name() != "containerlab") {
402: 		return nil
403: 	}
404: 
405: 	// inspect and destroy commands with --all flag don't use file find functionality
406: 	if (cobraCmd.Name() == "inspect" || cobraCmd.Name() == "destroy") &&
407: 		cobraCmd.Flag("all") != nil &&
408: 		cobraCmd.Flag("all").Value.String() == "true" {
409: 		return nil
410: 	}
411: 
412: 	var err error
413: 	// perform topology clone/fetch if the topo file is not available locally
414: 	if !clabutils.FileOrDirExists(o.Global.TopologyFile) {
415: 		switch {
416: 		case clabgit.IsGitHubOrGitLabURL(o.Global.TopologyFile) ||
417: 			clabgit.IsGitHubShortURL(o.Global.TopologyFile):
418: 			o.Global.TopologyFile, err = processGitTopoFile(o.Global.TopologyFile)
419: 			if err != nil {
420: 				return err
421: 			}
422: 		case clabutils.IsHttpURL(o.Global.TopologyFile, true):
423: 			// canonize the passed topo as URL by adding https schema if it was missing
424: 			if !strings.HasPrefix(o.Global.TopologyFile, "http://") &&
425: 				!strings.HasPrefix(o.Global.TopologyFile, "https://") {
426: 				o.Global.TopologyFile = "https://" + o.Global.TopologyFile
427: 			}
428: 		}
429: 	}
430: 
431: 	// if topo or name flags have been provided, don't try to derive the topo file
432: 	if o.Global.TopologyFile != "" || o.Global.TopologyName != "" {
433: 		return nil
434: 	}
435: 
436: 	log.Debugf("trying to find topology files automatically")
437: 
438: 	files, err := filepath.Glob("*.clab.y*ml")
439: 
440: 	if len(files) == 0 {
441: 		return errors.New("no topology files matching the pattern *.clab.yml or *.clab.yaml found")
442: 	}
443: 
444: 	if len(files) > 1 {
445: 		if !term.IsTerminal(os.Stdin.Fd()) {
446: 			return fmt.Errorf(
447: 				"more than one topology file matching the pattern *.clab.yml or *.clab.yaml found, "+
448: 					"can't pick one: %q",
449: 				files,
450: 			)
451: 		}
452: 
453: 		running := runningTopoFiles(cobraCmd.Context(), o)
454: 
455: 		selected, err := selectTopoFile(files, running)
456: 		if err != nil {
457: 			return err
458: 		}
459: 
460: 		o.Global.TopologyFile = selected
461: 
462: 		log.Info("Using topology file", "file", selected)
463: 
464: 		return nil
465: 	}
466: 
467: 	o.Global.TopologyFile = files[0]
468: 
469: 	log.Debugf("topology file found: %s", files[0])
470: 
471: 	return err
472: }
473: 
474: func processGitTopoFile(topo string) (string, error) {
475: 	// for short github urls, prepend https://github.com
476: 	// note that short notation only works for github links
477: 	if clabgit.IsGitHubShortURL(topo) {
478: 		topo = "https://github.com/" + topo
479: 	}
480: 
481: 	repo, err := clabgit.NewRepo(topo)
482: 	if err != nil {
483: 		return "", err
484: 	}
485: 
486: 	// Instantiate the git implementation to use.
487: 	gitImpl := clabgit.NewGoGit(repo)
488: 
489: 	// clone the repo via the Git Implementation
490: 	err = gitImpl.Clone()
491: 	if err != nil {
492: 		return "", err
493: 	}
494: 
495: 	// adjust permissions for the checked out repo
496: 	// it would belong to root/root otherwise
497: 	err = clabutils.SetUIDAndGID(repo.GetName())
498: 	if err != nil {
499: 		log.Errorf("error adjusting repository permissions %v. Continuing anyways", err)
500: 	}
501: 
502: 	// prepare the path with the repo based path
503: 	path := filepath.Join(repo.GetPath()...)
504: 	// prepend that path with the repo base directory
505: 	path = filepath.Join(repo.GetName(), path)
506: 
507: 	// change dir to the
508: 	err = os.Chdir(path)
509: 	if err != nil {
510: 		return "", err
511: 	}
512: 
513: 	return repo.GetFilename(), err
514: }

{\n\t\treturn true\n\t}\n\n\treturn v.IsSet(flagName)\n}\n\n// getTopoFilePath finds *.clab.y*ml file in the current working directory\n// if the file was not specified.\n// If the topology file // skipcq: GO-R1005\n\t// set commands which may use topo file find functionality, the rest don't need it\n\tif cobraCmd.Name() != \"deploy\" &&\n\t\tcobraCmd.Name() != \"destroy\" &&\n\t\tcobraCmd.Name() != \"save\" &&\n\t\tcobraCmd.Name() != \"graph\" &&\n\t\tcobraCmd.Name() != \"validate\" &&\n\t\tcobraCmd.Name() != \"logs\" &&\n\t\tcobraCmd.Name() != \"capture\" 4. Whether `tui --demo` truly doesn't use runtime; also TUI without demo might not need root either but that's conservative.
5. Whether `graph` without any of dot/mermaid/offline defaults to serving from containers — yes it starts a web server using topology from running lab.
6. `validate` — check whether validate from file uses runtime. Need to look at validate.go.
7. `version upgrade` — not in list, requires root? check.
8. Check `getCommandPath` implementation.
9. Check tests use `optionsInstance`, `GetOptions`, `updateOptionsFromViper` — check these exist in cmd package.
10. Check base files for root.go and root_test.go for context.
11. Check docs list consistency: "tui with --demo" listed as runtime-independent — matches code. But docs list "help" — in code "help" is in the map, ok. Docs don't mention `tools cert` root needing check? tools cert ca create — does it write to /root dir by default? Actually `tools cert ca create` default path... Let me check.

Also key concern: preRunFn runs before root privileges check; the ordering `DropRootPrivs()` then `CheckAndGetRootPrivs()`. The condition now skips CheckAndGetRootPrivs for runtime-independent commands. But DropRootPrivs drops root. So for runtime-independent commands, the process stays dropped (non-root). Fine.

But important: `getTopoFilePath(cobraCmd, o)` runs at the end of preRunFn for commands like graph/validate. For graph with a git URL topology, `processGitTopoFile` calls `clabutils.SetUIDAndGID(repo.GetName())` — "adjust permissions for the checked out repo it would belong to root/root otherwise" — that comment suggests it expects root. With podman (requires root) and graph --dot with a git URL, clab was previously run as root, cloned repo as root, then SetUIDAndGID changes ownership. Now running as non-root, the clone would be done as the user, so SetUIDAndGID may fail (log error and continue) — permissions would be user-owned anyway, so fine. Not a real defect.

Bigger issue: `getTopoFilePath` with `selectTopoFile` uses `runningTopoFiles(cobraCmd.Context(), o)` which lists running containers — uses runtime! For graph with multiple topo files in dir and no --topo... but wait, that path is only when TopologyFile and TopologyName are empty. `topologyFromFile` returns true when TopologyName == "". So `clab graph --dot` in a dir with multiple topology files and no name would be treated as runtime-independent (no root), but then getTopoFilePath calls runningTopoFiles which lists containers via runtime (podman rootless as user might work if user is in podman group...). Actually with podman requiring root in containerlab's model, listing containers would fail. Hmm, but is that realistic? `clab graph --dot` with multiple topo files in cwd. runningTopoFiles would call runtime ListContainers. With podman as non-root, the podman socket may not be accessible. This could produce an error. Edge case, but worth checking runningTopoFiles.

Let me look at the relevant files: graph.go, validate.go, tui.go, version.go, generate.go, tools cert files, completion.go, and getCommandPath.

8: 	"slices"

13: 	"github.com/spf13/viper"
14: )

16: const (
17: 	envPrefix = "CLAB"
18: )
19: 
20: var v *viper.Viper //nolint:gochecknoglobals
21: 
22: // initViper initializes viper for environment variable and config file support.
23: func initViper(cmd *cobra.Command) error {
24: 	v = viper.New()
25: 
26: 	// Set the environment variable prefix
27: 	v.SetEnvPrefix(envPrefix)
28: 
29: 	// Replace hyphens, slashes, and dots with underscores in environment variable names
30: 	// This allows keys like "deploy.graph" to match env var "CLAB_DEPLOY_GRAPH"
31: 	v.SetEnvKeyReplacer(strings.NewReplacer("-", "_", "/", "_", ".", "_"))
32: 
33: 	// Automatically bind environment variables
34: 	v.AutomaticEnv()
35: 
36: 	// Bind all flags to viper
37: 	return bindFlags(cmd, v)
38: }
39: 
40: // bindFlags binds all cobra flags to viper for a command and its subcommands.
41: // It uses command hierarchy to create namespaced keys for environment variables.
42: // For example, the --container flag in "tools disable-tx-offload" becomes:
43: // CLAB_TOOLS_DISABLE_TX_OFFLOAD_CONTAINER.
44: func bindFlags(cmd *cobra.Command, v *viper.Viper) error {
45: 	return bindFlagsWithPath(cmd, v, "")
46: }
47: 
48: // bindFlagsWithPath recursively binds flags with their command path as prefix.
49: func bindFlagsWithPath(cmd *cobra.Command, v *viper.Viper, cmdPath string) error {
50: 	// Build the current command path
51: 	currentPath := cmdPath
52: 	isRootCmd := cmd.Name() == "containerlab" || cmd.Name() == ""
53: 
54: 	if !isRootCmd {
55: 		if currentPath != "" {
56: 			currentPath = currentPath + "." + cmd.Name()
57: 		} else {
58: 			currentPath = cmd.Name()
59: 		}
60: 	}
61: 
62: 	// Bind persistent flags
63: 	cmd.PersistentFlags().VisitAll(func(flag *pflag.Flag) {
64: 		// For root command persistent flags, bind WITHOUT prefix so they work globally
65: 		// as CLAB_<FLAG> from any command
66: 		if isRootCmd {
67: 			_ = v.BindPFlag(flag.Name, flag)
68: 		}
69: 
70: 		// Also bind with command path if we have one (for subcommands)
71: 		if currentPath != "" {
72: 			key := currentPath + "." + flag.Name
73: 			_ = v.BindPFlag(key, flag)
74: 		}
75: 	})
76: 
77: 	// Bind local flags with command path prefix
78: 	cmd.Flags().VisitAll(func(flag *pflag.Flag) {
79: 		// Skip if this flag is a persistent flag (already bound above)
80: 		if cmd.PersistentFlags().Lookup(flag.Name) != nil {
81: 			return
82: 		}
83: 
84: 		// Local flags MUST have a command path prefix
85: 		// Don't bind without prefix to avoid ambiguity
86: 		if currentPath != "" {
87: 			key := currentPath + "." + flag.Name
88: 			_ = v.BindPFlag(key, flag)
89: 		}
90: 	})
91: 
92: 	// Recursively bind flags for all subcommands
93: 	for _, subCmd := range cmd.Commands() {
94: 		if err := bindFlagsWithPath(subCmd, v, currentPath); err != nil {
95: 			return err
96: 		}
97: 	}
98: 
99: 	return nil
100: }
101: 
102: // updateOptionsFromViper updates the Options struct from viper values
103: // when environment variables are set and flags are not explicitly provided.
104: // This iterates through all flags that were bound to viper and updates their
105: // values from environment variables if the flag was not explicitly set.
106: func updateOptionsFromViper(cmd *cobra.Command, _ *Options) {
107: 	// Build command path for this command
108: 	cmdPath := getCommandPath(cmd)
109: 
110: 	// Collect all flags that should be checked (avoid duplicates)
111: 	flagMap := make(map[string]*pflag.Flag)
112: 
113: 	// Helper to add flags to map
114: 	addFlags := func(fs *pflag.FlagSet) {
115: 		fs.VisitAll(func(f *pflag.Flag) {
116: 			if _, exists := flagMap[f.Name]; !exists {
117: 				flagMap[f.Name] = f
118: 			}
119: 		})
120: 	}
121: 
122: 	// Add this command's local flags
123: 	addFlags(cmd.Flags())
124: 
125: 	// Add this command's persistent flags
126: 	addFlags(cmd.PersistentFlags())
127: 
128: 	// Add inherited persistent flags from all parent commands
129: 	parent := cmd.Parent()
130: 	for parent != nil {
131: 		addFlags(parent.PersistentFlags())
132: 		parent = parent.Parent()
133: 	}
134: 
135: 	// Now update all collected flags from viper
136: 	for _, f := range flagMap {
137: 		updateFlagFromViper(f, cmdPath)
138: 	}
139: }
140: 
141: // getCommandPath builds the command path from root to current command.
142: // For example: "tools.disable-tx-offload".
143: func getCommandPath(cmd *cobra.Command) string {
144: 	var parts []string
145: 
146: 	current := cmd
147: 
148: 	for current != nil && current.Name() != "containerlab" && current.Name() != "" {
149: 		parts = append([]string{current.Name()}, parts...)
150: 		current = current.Parent()
151: 	}
152: 
153: 	return strings.Join(parts, ".")
154: }
155: 
156: // updateFlagFromViper updates a single flag's value from viper if:
157: // - The flag was not explicitly set on the command line
158: // - Viper has a value for this flag (from env var or config file).
159: // It checks for the value using the command-namespaced key.
160: func updateFlagFromViper(f *pflag.Flag, cmdPath string) {
161: 	// Skip if flag was explicitly set via command line
162: 	if f.Changed {
163: 		return
164: 	}
165: 
166: 	// Determine the key to check in viper
167: 	// Strategy:
168: 	// 1. First check if flag is bound with full command path (e.g., version.short)
169: 	// 2. If not found AND the flag name itself is a bound key (root persistent flags),
170: 	//    then check that key
171: 	var key string
172: 
173: 	var hasValue bool
174: 
175: 	if cmdPath != "" {
176: 		// For commands with a path, first try the full command.flag format
177: 		key = cmdPath + "." + f.Name
178: 		hasValue = v.IsSet(key)
179: 
180: 		// If not found with command path, check if this flag name is bound at root level
181: 		// This happens for root persistent flags which are bound without prefix
182: 		if !hasValue {
183: 			// Check if the flag name itself is a viper key (indicates root persistent flag)
184: 			// We use AllKeys to check all bound keys
185: 			isRootKey := slices.Contains(v.AllKeys(), f.Name)
186: 
187: 			// Only fall back to unprefixed key if it's actually bound at root
188: 			if isRootKey {
189: 				key = f.Name
190: 				hasValue = v.IsSet(key)
191: 			}
192: 		}
193: 	} else {
194: 		// For commands without a path (shouldn't normally happen)
195: 		key = f.Name
196: 		hasValue = v.IsSet(key)
197: 	}
198: 
199: 	// Skip if viper doesn't have a value for this flag
200: 	if !hasValue {
201: 		return
202: 	}
203: 
204: 	// Get the value from viper based on the flag's type and set it
205: 	// The flag.Value.Set() method handles type conversion for us
206: 	var val string
207: 
208: 	switch f.Value.Type() {
209: 	case "bool":
210: 		val = v.GetString(key)
211: 	case "string":
212: 		val = v.GetString(key)
213: 	case "stringSlice":
214: 		// For slices, join with comma as that's what cobra expects
215: 		slice := v.GetStringSlice(key)
216: 		if len(slice) > 0 {
217: 			val = strings.Join(slice, ",")
218: 		}
219: 	case "int", "int8", "int16", "int32", "int64":
220: 		val = v.GetString(key)
221: 	case "uint", "uint8", "uint16", "uint32", "uint64":
222: 		val = v.GetString(key)
223: 	case "float32", "float64":
224: 		val = v.GetString(key)
225: 	case "duration":
226: 		val = v.GetString(key)
227: 	case "count":
228: 		val = v.GetString(key)
229: 	default:
230: 		// For any other type, try to get it as a string
231: 		val = v.GetString(key)
232: 	}
233: 
234: 	// Set the value on the flag (which updates the underlying variable)
235: 	if val != "" {
236: 		_ = f.Value.Set(val)
237: 	}
238: }

(End of file - total 238 lines)

21: func graphCmd(o *Options) (*cobra.Command, error) {
22: 	c := &cobra.Command{
23: 		Use:   "graph",
24: 		Short: "generate a topology graph",
25: 		Long: "generate topology graph based on the topology definition file and " +
26: 			"running containers\nreference: https://containerlab.dev/cmd/graph/",
27: 		RunE: func(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
28: 			return graphFn(cobraCmd.Context(), o)
29: 		},
30: 	}
31: 
32: 	c.Flags().StringVarP(
33: 		&o.Graph.Server,
34: 		"srv",
35: 		"s",
36: 		o.Graph.Server,
37: 		"HTTP server address serving the topology view",
38: 	)
39: 	c.Flags().BoolVarP(
40: 		&o.Graph.Offline,
41: 		"offline",
42: 		"o",
43: 		o.Graph.Offline,
44: 		"use only information from topo file when building graph",
45: 	)
46: 	c.Flags().BoolVarP(
47: 		&o.Graph.GenerateDotFile,
48: 		"dot",
49: 		"",
50: 		o.Graph.GenerateDotFile,
51: 		"generate dot file",
52: 	)
53: 	c.Flags().BoolVarP(
54: 		&o.Graph.GenerateMermaid,
55: 		"mermaid",
56: 		"",
57: 		o.Graph.GenerateMermaid,
58: 		"print mermaid flowchart to stdout",
59: 	)
60: 	c.Flags().StringVarP(
61: 		&o.Graph.MermaidDirection,
62: 		"mermaid-direction", "",
63: 		o.Graph.MermaidDirection,
64: 		"specify direction of mermaid diagram",
65: 	)
66: 	c.Flags().StringSliceVar(
67: 		&o.Graph.DrawIOArgs,
68: 		"drawio-args",
69: 		o.Graph.DrawIOArgs,
70: 		"Additional flags to pass to the drawio diagram generation tool "+
71: 			"(can be specified multiple times)",
72: 	)
73: 	c.Flags().BoolVarP(
74: 		&o.Graph.GenerateDrawIO,
75: 		"drawio",
76: 		"",
77: 		o.Graph.GenerateDrawIO,
78: 		"generate drawio diagram file",
79: 	)
80: 	c.Flags().StringVarP(
81: 		&o.Graph.DrawIOVersion,
82: 		"drawio-version",
83: 		"",
84: 		o.Graph.DrawIOVersion,
85: 		"version of the clab-io-draw container to use for generating drawio diagram file",
86: 	)
87: 	c.Flags().StringVarP(
88: 		&o.Graph.Template,
89: 		"template",
90: 		"",
91: 		o.Graph.Template,
92: 		"Go html template used to generate the graph",
93: 	)
94: 	c.Flags().StringVarP(
95: 		&o.Graph.StaticDirectory,
96: 		"static-dir",
97: 		"",
98: 		o.Graph.StaticDirectory,
99: 		"Serve static files from the specified directory",
100: 	)
101: 	c.Flags().StringSliceVarP(
102: 		&o.Filter.NodeFilter,
103: 		"node-filter",
104: 		"",
105: 		o.Filter.NodeFilter,
106: 		"comma separated list of nodes to include",
107: 	)
108: 	c.MarkFlagsMutuallyExclusive("dot", "mermaid", "drawio")
109: 
110: 	return c, nil
111: }
112: 
113: func graphFn(ctx context.Context, o *Options) error {
114: 	c, err := clabcore.NewContainerLab(o.ToClabOptions()...)
115: 	if err != nil {
116: 		return err
117: 	}
118: 
119: 	// Lab runtimes do not build the topology's nodes; the graph modes they support render
120: 	// from the topology file, so the nodes are built here without a container runtime.
121: 	if err := c.LoadTopologyNodes(); err != nil {
122: 		return err
123: 	}
124: 
125: 	err = c.ResolveLinks()
126: 	if err != nil {
127: 		return err
128: 	}
129: 
130: 	if o.Graph.GenerateDotFile {
131: 		return c.GenerateDotGraph(ctx)
132: 	}
133: 
134: 	if o.Graph.GenerateMermaid {
135: 		return c.GenerateMermaidGraph(o.Graph.MermaidDirection)
136: 	}
137: 
138: 	if o.Graph.GenerateDrawIO {
139: 		return c.GenerateDrawioDiagram(o.Graph.DrawIOVersion, o.Graph.DrawIOArgs)
140: 	}
141: 
142: 	gtopo := clabcore.GraphTopo{
143: 		Nodes: make([]clabtypes.ContainerDetails, 0, len(c.Nodes)),
144: 		Links: make([]clabcore.Link, 0, len(c.Links)),
145: 	}
146: 
147: 	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
148: 	defer cancel()
149: 
150: 	var containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
151: 	// if offline mode is not enforced, list containers matching lab name
152: 	if !o.Graph.Offline {
153: 		containers, err = c.ListContainers(ctx,
154: 			clabcore.WithListLabName(c.Config.Name))
155: 		if err != nil {
156: 			return err
157: 		}
158: 
159: 		log.Debugf("found %d containers", len(containers))
160: 	}
161: 
162: 	switch {
163: 	case len(containers) == 0:
164: 		c.BuildGraphFromTopo(&gtopo)
165: 	case len(containers) > 0:
166: 		c.BuildGraphFromDeployedLab(&gtopo, containers)
167: 	}
168: 
169: 	sort.Slice(gtopo.Nodes, func(i, j int) bool {
170: 		return gtopo.Nodes[i].Name < gtopo.Nodes[j].Name
171: 	})
172: 
173: 	for _, l := range c.Links {
174: 		eps := l.GetEndpoints()
175: 		// Skip links that are not point-to-point (e.g. dummy, host, macvlan,
176: 		// mgmt-net links expose a single endpoint). Indexing eps[1] on such
177: 		// links would panic, so they are not rendered as edges.
178: 		if len(eps) != 2 {
179: 			log.Debugf(
180: 				"skipping non point-to-point link with %d endpoint(s) in graph topology",
181: 				len(eps),
182: 			)
183: 			continue
184: 		}
185: 
186: 		ifaceDisplayNameA := eps[0].GetIfaceDisplayName()
187: 		ifaceDisplayNameB := eps[1].GetIfaceDisplayName()
188: 
189: 		gtopo.Links = append(gtopo.Links, clabcore.Link{
190: 			Source:         eps[0].GetNode().GetShortName(),
191: 			SourceEndpoint: ifaceDisplayNameA,
192: 			Target:         eps[1].GetNode().GetShortName(),
193: 			TargetEndpoint: ifaceDisplayNameB,
194: 		})
195: 	}
196: 
197: 	b, err := json.Marshal(gtopo)
198: 	if err != nil {
199: 		return err
200: 	}
201: 
202: 	log.Debugf("generating graph using data: %s", string(b))
203: 	topoD := clabcore.TopoData{
204: 		Name: c.Config.Name,
205: 		Data: template.JS(string(b)), // skipcq: GSC-G203
206: 	}
207: 
208: 	return c.ServeTopoGraph(o.Graph.Template, o.Graph.StaticDirectory, o.Graph.Server, topoD)
209: }

(End of file - total 209 lines)

Reasoning
Interesting. `graph --offline` with `--name` alone: The code treats `graph --offline --name lab` (no topo file) as NOT runtime-independent since `topologyFromFile` requires TopologyFile != "" or TopologyName == "". Good, because offline with --name only... wait, `graph --offline --name lab` — offline mode only uses topo file info. But the topology file is required to load nodes at all — `LoadTopologyNodes`. If only name is given, LoadTopologyNodes would fail? Actually for graph, `getTopoFilePath` runs for graph command: if TopologyFile and TopologyName both empty, finds topo files in cwd. So graph with `--name` alone reads topology from... hmm. Actually `c.LoadTopologyNodes()` parses a topo file. With --name alone, the topo file is missing — how does graph work with just --name? Let me check `LoadTopologyNodes` in core. Actually the docs say graph "not with --name alone" — matches `topologyFromFile`. OK.

Wait — but there's a subtlety: `graph --offline` with `--name` but ALSO a topo file in cwd? getTopoFilePath: if TopologyFile != "" or TopologyName != "" → return nil early (line 432). So with --name set and no --topo, TopologyFile remains "" — then LoadTopologyNodes with empty topo file? Let's check core.NewContainerLab and LoadTopologyNodes to see what happens with --name alone. Not directly relevant to this PR though; the PR's condition matches documented behavior.

Now, the key question about `graph --dot --name lab -t lab.clab.yml`: topologyFromFile returns true (TopoFile != ""). Good.

Edge: `graph --dot --name lab` (name only, no -t): TopologyFile == "" and TopologyName != "" → topologyFromFile false → not runtime independent → requires root with podman. But actually with --dot and --name, does graphFn need the runtime? `LoadTopologyNodes` — need to check whether it uses runtime when topo file is empty. Let me check core.

Also `validate`: check validate.go.

And `tui --demo`: check tui.go.

But wait: `updateFlagFromViper` skips setting when `val == ""` (line 235). For bool flags with env value "false" — CLAB_GRAPH_OFFLINE=false: val = "false", Set("false") → false. OK.

Another candidate issue: `DropRootPrivs()` happens before the check. Fine.

Bigger candidate: `commandIsRuntimeIndependent` is evaluated in `PersistentPreRunE` of the root command. For the `__complete` hidden command, PersistentPreRunE — does cobra run PersistentPreRunE for `__complete`? Yes, `__complete` is a regular command added at execution; cobra's completion command `__complete` has its own Run and PersistentPreRunE from root applies? Actually for `__complete`, cobra documentation: "the completion commands are added with `InitDefaultCompletionCmd`... `__complete` command's PersistentPreRunE? The root's PersistentPreRunE runs for any command that doesn't define its own PreRun. The `__complete` command added by cobra does not define PersistentPreRunE, so root's runs. That's why they added `cobra.ShellCompRequestCmd` to the list. OK.

Now potential real issue #1: `getTopoFilePath` runs after the root-privilege branch, and for `graph`/`validate` with a git URL topology or directory-glob flow, there's `runningTopoFiles(cobraCmd.Context(), o)` which lists containers via the runtime. Consider `clab graph --dot` with podman (requires root per model), multiple topo files in cwd, no --topo/--name. `topologyFromFile` → TopologyName == "" → true, and --dot set → runtime-independent → no root. Let me check `runningTopoFiles` in topo_select.go.

15: func selectTopoFile(files []string, running map[string]bool) (string, error) {
16: 	items := make([]clabutilsselector.Item, len(files))
17: 
18: 	for i, f := range files {
19: 		note := ""

22: 		}
23: 
24: 		items[i] = clabutilsselector.Item{Label: f, Note: note}
25: 	}

27: 	idx, err := clabutilsselector.FromList("Multiple topology files found, select one:", items)
28: 	if err != nil {
29: 		return "", err
30: 	}
31: 
32: 	return files[idx], nil
33: }
34: 
35: // runningTopoFiles returns the set of absolute topology-file paths that currently
36: // have deployed containers (keyed by the clab-topo-file label). It is best-effort:
37: // on any error (e.g. the runtime is unavailable) it returns an empty set so the
38: // picker still works, just without "(running)" annotations.
39: func runningTopoFiles(ctx context.Context, o *Options) map[string]bool {

44: 		return running

54: 			running[topo] = true
55: 		}
56: 	}
57: 
58: 	return running
59: }

180: 
181: 	return c, nil

184: func generate(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, o *Options, reg *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) error {
185: 	if o.Global.TopologyName == "" {
186: 		return errors.New("provide a lab name with --name flag")
187: 	}
188: 
189: 	licenses, err := parseFlag(kind, license)
190: 	if err != nil {
191: 		return err
192: 	}
193:

195:

197: 	if err != nil {
198: 		return err
199: 	}
200:

202: 
203: 	nodeDefs, err := parseNodesFlag(kind, nodesFlag...)
204: 	if err != nil {
205: 		return err
206: 	}
207: 
208: 	log.Debugf("parsed nodes definitions: %+v", nodeDefs)
209: 
210: 	b, err := generateTopologyConfig(
211: 		o.Global.TopologyName,
212: 		o.Deploy.ManagementNetworkName,
213: 		o.Deploy.ManagementIPv4Subnet.String(),
214: 		o.Deploy.ManagementIPv6Subnet.String(),
215: 		images,
216: 		licenses,
217: 		reg,
218: 		nodeDefs...)
219: 	if err != nil {
220: 		return err
221: 	}
222: 
223: 	log.Debugf("generated topo: %s", string(b))
224: 
225: 	if file != "" {
226: 		err = clabutils.CreateFile(file, string(b))
227: 		if err != nil {
228: 			return err
229: 		}
230: 	}
231: 
232: 	if deploy {
233: 		err = clabutils.CheckAndGetRootPrivs()
234: 		if err != nil {
235: 			return err
236: 		}
237: 
238: 		o.Deploy.Reconfigure = true
239: 		if file == "" {
240: 			file = fmt.Sprintf("%s.clab.yml", o.Global.TopologyName)
241: 
242: 			err = clabutils.CreateFile(file, string(b))
243: 			if err != nil {
244: 				return err
245: 			}
246: 		}
247: 
248: 		o.Global.TopologyFile = file
249: 
250: 		// Pass owner to deploy command if specified
251: 		if o.Deploy.LabOwner != "" {
252: 			// This will be picked up by the deploy command
253: 			os.Setenv("CLAB_OWNER", o.Deploy.LabOwner)
254: 		}
255: 
256: 		return deployFn(cobraCmd, o)
257: 	}
258: 
259: 	if file == "" {
260: 		fmt.Println(string(b))
261: 	}
262: 
263: 	return nil
264: }
265: 
266: func generateTopologyConfig( //nolint: funlen
267: 	name,
268: 	network,
269: 	ipv4range,
270: 	ipv6range string,
271: 	images,
272: 	licenses map[string]string,
273: 	reg *clabnodes.NodeRegistry,
274: 	nodes ...nodesDef,
275: ) ([]byte, error) {
276: 	numStages := len(nodes)
277: 	config := &clabcore.Config{
278: 		Name: name,
279: 		Mgmt: new(clabtypes.MgmtNet),
280: 		Topology: &clabtypes.Topology{
281: 			Kinds: make(map[string]*clabtypes.NodeDefinition),
282: 			Nodes: make(map[string]*clabtypes.NodeDefinition),
283: 		},
284: 	}
285: 
286: 	config.MgmtNetworks = clabtypes.MgmtNetworks{config.Mgmt}
287: 	config.Mgmt.Network = network
288: 
289: 	if ipv4range != clabconstants.UnsetNetAddr {
290: 		config.Mgmt.IPv4Subnet = ipv4range
291: 	}
292: 
293: 	if ipv6range != clabconstants.UnsetNetAddr {
294: 		config.Mgmt.IPv6Subnet = ipv6range
295: 	}
296: 
297: 	for k, img := range images {
298: 		config.Topology.Kinds[k] = &clabtypes.NodeDefinition{Image: img}
299: 	}
300: 
301: 	for k, lic := range licenses {
302: 		if knd, ok := config.Topology.Kinds[k]; ok {
303: 			knd.License = lic
304: 			config.Topology.Kinds[k] = knd
305: 
306: 			continue
307: 		}
308: 
309: 		config.Topology.Kinds[k] = &clabtypes.NodeDefinition{License: lic}
310: 	}
311: 
312: 	if numStages == 1 {
313: 		for j := range nodes[0].numNodes {
314: 			node1 := fmt.Sprintf("%s1-%d", nodePrefix, j+1)
315: 			if _, ok := config.Topology.Nodes[node1]; !ok {
316: 				config.Topology.Nodes[node1] = &clabtypes.NodeDefinition{
317: 					Group: fmt.Sprintf("%s-1", groupPrefix),
318: 					Kind:  nodes[0].kind,
319: 					Type:  nodes[0].typ,
320: 				}
321: 			}
322: 		}
323: 	}
324: 
325: 	generateNodesAttributes := reg.GetGenerateNodeAttributes()
326: 
327: 	for i := range numStages - 1 {
328: 		interfaceOffset := uint(0)
329: 		if i > 0 {
330: 			interfaceOffset = nodes[i-1].numNodes
331: 		}
332: 
333: 		for j := range nodes[i].numNodes {
334: 			node1 := fmt.Sprintf("%s%d-%d", nodePrefix, i+1, j+1)
335: 			if _, ok := config.Topology.Nodes[node1]; !ok {
336: 				config.Topology.Nodes[node1] = &clabtypes.NodeDefinition{
337: 					Group: fmt.Sprintf("%s-%d", groupPrefix, i+1),
338: 					Kind:  nodes[i].kind,
339: 					Type:  nodes[i].typ,
340: 				}
341: 			}
342: 
343: 			for k := range nodes[i+1].numNodes {
344: 				node2 := fmt.Sprintf("%s%d-%d", nodePrefix, i+2, k+1) //nolint: mnd
345: 				if _, ok := config.Topology.Nodes[node2]; !ok {
346: 					config.Topology.Nodes[node2] = &clabtypes.NodeDefinition{
347: 						Group: fmt.Sprintf("%s-%d", groupPrefix, i+2), //nolint: mnd
348: 						Kind:  nodes[i+1].kind,
349: 						Type:  nodes[i+1].typ,
350: 					}
351: 				}
352: 
353: 				// create a raw veth link
354: 				l := &clablinks.LinkVEthRaw{
355: 					Endpoints: []*clablinks.EndpointRaw{
356: 						clablinks.NewEndpointRaw(node1, fmt.Sprintf(
357: 							generateNodesAttributes[nodes[i].kind].GetInterfaceFormat(),
358: 							k+1+interfaceOffset,
359: 						), ""),
360: 						clablinks.NewEndpointRaw(node2, fmt.Sprintf(
361: 							generateNodesAttributes[nodes[i+1].kind].GetInterfaceFormat(),
362: 							j+1,
363: 						), ""),
364: 					},
365: 				}
366: 
367: 				// encapsulate the brief rawlink in a linkdefinition
368: 				ld := &clablinks.LinkDefinition{
369: 					Link: l.ToLinkBriefRaw(),
370: 				}
371: 
372: 				// add the link to the topology
373: 				config.Topology.Links = append(config.Topology.Links, ld)
374: 			}
375: 		}
376: 	}
377: 
378: 	return yaml.Marshal(config)
379: }
380: 
381: func parseFlag(kind string, ls []string) (map[string]string, error) {
382: 	result := make(map[string]string)
383: 
384: 	for _, l := range ls {
385: 		items := strings.SplitN(l, "=", 2) //nolint: mnd
386: 
387: 		switch len(items) {
388: 		case 0:
389: 			log.Errorf("missing value for flag item '%s'", l)
390: 
391: 			return nil, errSyntax
392: 		case 1:
393: 			if kind == "" {
394: 				log.Errorf("no kind specified for flag item '%s'", l)
395: 
396: 				return nil, errSyntax
397: 			}
398: 
399: 			if _, ok := result[kind]; !ok {
400: 				result[kind] = items[0]
401: 			} else {
402: 				log.Errorf("duplicated flag item for kind '%s'", kind)
403: 
404: 				return nil, errDuplicatedValue
405: 			}
406: 		case 2: //nolint: mnd
407: 			if _, ok := result[items[0]]; !ok {
408: 				result[items[0]] = items[1]
409: 			} else {
410: 				log.Errorf("duplicated flag item for kind '%s'", items[0])
411: 
412: 				return nil, errDuplicatedValue
413: 			}
414: 		}
415: 	}
416: 
417: 	return result, nil
418: }
419: 
420: func parseNodesFlag(kind string, nodes ...string) ([]nodesDef, error) {
421: 	numStages := len(nodes)
422: 	if numStages == 0 {
423: 		log.Error("no nodes specified using --nodes")
424: 
425: 		return nil, errSyntax
426: 	}
427: 
428: 	result := make([]nodesDef, numStages)
429: 	for idx, n := range nodes {
430: 		def := nodesDef{}
431: 
432: 		items := strings.SplitN(n, ":", nodeFlagNumKindTypePartCount)
433: 		if len(items) == 0 {
434: 			log.Errorf("wrong --nodes format '%s'", n)
435: 
436: 			return nil, errSyntax
437: 		}
438: 
439: 		i, err := strconv.Atoi(items[0])
440: 		if err != nil {
441: 			log.Errorf("failed converting '%s' to an integer: %v", items[0], err)
442: 
443: 			return nil, errSyntax
444: 		}
445: 
446: 		def.numNodes = uint(i)
447: 
448: 		switch len(items) {
449: 		// kind is assumed to be `srl` or set with --kind
450: 		case nodeFlagNumPartCount:
451: 			if kind == "" {
452: 				log.Errorf("no kind specified for nodes '%s'", n)
453: 				return nil, errSyntax
454: 			}
455: 
456: 			def.kind = kind
457: 		case nodeFlagNumKindPartCount:
458: 			if kind == "" {
459: 				log.Errorf("no kind specified for nodes '%s'", n)
460: 				return nil, errSyntax
461: 			}
462: 
463: 			def.kind = items[1]
464: 		case nodeFlagNumKindTypePartCount:
465: 			def.numNodes = uint(i)
466: 			def.kind = kind
467: 
468: 			if items[1] != "" {
469: 				def.kind = items[1]
470: 			}
471: 
472: 			def.typ = items[2]
473: 		}
474: 
475: 		result[idx] = def
476: 	}
477: 
478: 	return result, nil
479: }

make(map[string]string)\n\n\tfor _, l := range ls {\n\t\titems := strings.SplitN(l, \"=\", 2) //nolint: mnd\n\n\t\tswitch len(items) {\n\t\tcase 0:\n\t\t\tlog.Errorf(\"missing value for flag item '%s'\", l)\n\n\t\t\treturn nil, errSyntax\n\t\tcase 1:\n\t\t\tif kind == \"\" {\n\t\t\t\tlog.Errorf(\"no kind specified for flag item '%s'\", l)\n\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, errSyntax\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tif errDuplicatedValue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\tcase 2: //nolint: mnd\n\t\t\tif _, ok := result[items[0]]; !ok {\n\t\t\t\tresult[items[0]] = items[1]\n\t\t\t} else {\n\t\t\t\tlog.Errorf(\"duplicated flag item for kind '%s'\", items[0])\n\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, errDuplicatedValue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn result, nil\n}\n\nfunc parseNodesFlag(kind string, nodes ...string) ([]nodesDef, error) {\n\tnumStages {\n\t\tdef := nodesDef{}\n\n\t\titems := strings.SplitN(n, \":\", nodeFlagNumKindTypePartCount)\n\t\tif len(items) == 0 {\n\t\t\tlog.Errorf(\"wrong --nodes format '%s'\", n)\n\n\t\t\treturn nil, errSyntax\n\t\t}\n\n\t\ti, err := strconv.Atoi(items[0])\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Errorf(\"failed converting '%s' to an integer: %v\", items[0], err)\n\n\t\t\treturn nil, errSyntax\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tdef.numNodes = uint(i)\n\n\t\tswitch len(items) {\n\t\t// kind is assumed to be `srl` or set with --kind\n\t\tcase nodeFlagNumPartCount:\n\t\t\tif kind == \"\" Reasoning
Interesting — `validate` with a lab runtime (`IsLabRuntimeName`) calls `c.ValidateLabRuntimeTopology` which is a remote API call... but lab runtimes skip root anyway (`commandSkipsRoot`), so no issue.

`validate` from file with podman: `validateFn` — `NewContainerLab` — does it instantiate runtime? It says "without touching the runtime state". OK.

`generate --deploy` checks `CheckAndGetRootPrivs` itself (line 233). Good — comment accurate.

Now — an important note about `generate`: `generate` command requires `--name` and writes files. `runtimeIndependentCommands` includes "generate" — good.

But wait — `generate --deploy` with podman: `deploy` var is a package-level bool in generate.go, NOT part of `o`. `commandIsRuntimeIndependent` doesn't check the deploy flag for "generate" — but generate handles root itself. However, there's a subtlety: with podman, `generate --deploy` calls `deployFn` after getting root. Fine.

Now the graph flow concern: `graph --dot` with multiple topo files in cwd, podman runtime. `getTopoFilePath` is invoked after the root check. `runningTopoFiles` is best-effort: "on any error (e.g. the runtime is unavailable) it returns an empty set so the picker still works". So no failure — good, the guard exists. Also `NewContainerLab` may error → returns empty. OK, not a defect.

But wait — `selectTopoFile` only happens if `term.IsTerminal`. Fine.

Now check `tui.go` and `tools_cert.go`, `version.go`, `completion.go`. Also check options.go for `TUI.Demo`, `Graph.*` fields. Also check `DropRootPrivs` / `CheckAndGetRootPrivs` in utils.

94: // isInteractiveTerminal reports whether the process talks to a terminal a full-screen UI
95: // can run in.
96: func isInteractiveTerminal() bool {
97: 	return term.IsTerminal(os.Stdin.Fd()) && term.IsTerminal(os.Stdout.Fd()) &&
98: 		os.Getenv("TERM") != "dumb"
99: }
100: 
101: func tuiFn(ctx context.Context, o *Options) error {
102: 	if !isInteractiveTerminal() {
103: 		return errors.New("the TUI needs a terminal; use `containerlab inspect --all` " +
104: 			"to list labs from a script")
105: 	}
106: 
107: 	be, err := newTUIBackend(o)
108: 	if err != nil {
109: 		return err
110: 	}
111: 
112: 	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(ctx)
113: 	defer cancel()
114: 
115: 	var running atomic.Bool
116: 
117: 	restoreSignals := tuiSignals(cancel, running.Load)
118: 	defer restoreSignals()
119: 
120: 	restoreLog := silenceLogger()
121: 	defer restoreLog()
122: 
123: 	err = clabinternaltui.Run(ctx, be, clabinternaltui.Options{
124: 		Theme:    o.TUI.Theme,
125: 		Lab:      o.Global.TopologyName,
126: 		NoMouse:  o.TUI.NoMouse,
127: 		Running:  running.Store,
128: 		Graphics: o.TUI.Graphics,
129: 	})
130: 	if err != nil {
131: 		return fmt.Errorf("the TUI stopped: %w", err)
132: 	}
133: 
134: 	return nil
135: }

137: // newTUIBackend creates the data source of the TUI: the simulated labs of the demo mode,
138: // the container runtime of this host, or the lab runtime that was asked for.
139: func newTUIBackend(o *Options) (clabinternaltuibackend.Backend, error) {
140: 	if o.TUI.Demo {
141: 		return clabinternaltuibackendfake.New(clabinternaltuibackendfake.Options{
142: 			Live: true,
143: 			Seed: uint64(time.Now().UnixNano()), //nolint:gosec // any seed will do
144: 		}), nil
145: 	}
146: 
147: 	return clabinternaltuibackendlocal.New(clabinternaltuibackendlocal.Options{
148: 		Runtime:   o.Global.Runtime,
149: 		Namespace: o.Global.Namespace,
150: 		Timeout:   o.Global.Timeout,
151: 		LabsDirs:  o.TUI.LabsDirs,
152: 		ReadOnly:  o.TUI.ReadOnly,
153: 		Debug:     o.Global.DebugCount > 0,
154: 	})
155: }
156: 
157: // tuiSignals takes over the handling of termination signals while the TUI runs. The
158: // regular handler ends the process on the spot, which would leave the terminal in the
159: // state the UI put it in. running tells whether the UI owns the terminal. It returns a
160: // function that hands the signals back.
161: func tuiSignals(quit context.CancelFunc, running func() bool) (restore func()) {
162: 	handle := func(sig os.Signal) {
163: 		// While the UI owns the terminal, an interrupt can only come from outside of it,
164: 		// where ctrl+c is a key like any other: it is the terminal signaling the session
165: 		// a node shell runs in. That session deals with it, the UI carries on. Before
166: 		// and after, ie. while the labs are listed for the first time, it ends the UI.
167: 		if sig == syscall.SIGINT && running() {
168: 			return
169: 		}
170: 
171: 		quit()
172: 	}
173: 
174: 	signalDelegate.Store(&handle)
175: 
176: 	// A closed terminal ends the UI like a termination request does.
177: 	hangup := make(chan os.Signal, 1)
178: 	signal.Notify(hangup, syscall.SIGHUP)
179: 
180: 	done := make(chan struct{})
181: 
182: 	go func() {
183: 		select {
184: 		case <-hangup:
185: 			quit()
186: 		case <-done:
187: 		}
188: 	}()
189: 
190: 	return func() {
191: 		close(done)
192: 		signal.Stop(hangup)
193: 		signalDelegate.Store(nil)
194: 	}
195: }
196: 
197: // silenceLogger keeps log output off the screen while the TUI owns it. Whatever the
198: // packages used in-process log would otherwise be written across the UI. It returns a
199: // function that restores the logger.
200: func silenceLogger() (restore func()) {
201: 	log.SetOutput(io.Discard)
202: 
203: 	return func() { log.SetOutput(os.Stderr) }
204: }

189: // createCA creates a new CA certificate and key and writes them to the specified path.
190: func createCA(o *Options) error {
191: 	var err error
192: 	if o.ToolsCert.Path == "" {
193: 		o.ToolsCert.Path, err = os.Getwd()
194: 		if err != nil {
195: 			return err
196: 		}
197: 	}

199: 	log.Infof(
200: 		"Certificate attributes: CN=%s, C=%s, L=%s, O=%s, OU=%s, Validity period=%s",
201: 		o.ToolsCert.CommonName,
202: 		o.ToolsCert.Country,
203: 		o.ToolsCert.Locality,
204: 		o.ToolsCert.Organization,
205: 		o.ToolsCert.OrganizationUnit,
206: 		o.ToolsCert.Expiry,
207: 	)
208: 
209: 	ca := clabcert.NewCA()
210: 
211: 	expDuration, err := time.ParseDuration(o.ToolsCert.Expiry)
212: 	if err != nil {
213: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed parsing expiry %s", o.ToolsCert.Expiry)
214: 	}
215: 
216: 	csrInput := &clabcert.CACSRInput{
217: 		CommonName:       o.ToolsCert.CommonName,
218: 		Country:          o.ToolsCert.Country,
219: 		Locality:         o.ToolsCert.Locality,
220: 		Organization:     o.ToolsCert.Organization,
221: 		OrganizationUnit: o.ToolsCert.OrganizationUnit,
222: 		Expiry:           expDuration,
223: 		KeySize:          int(o.ToolsCert.KeySize),
224: 	}
225: 
226: 	caCert, err := ca.GenerateCACert(csrInput)
227: 	if err != nil {
228: 		return err
229: 	}
230: 
231: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(
232: 		o.ToolsCert.Path,
233: 		clabconstants.PermissionsOpen,
234: 	) // skipcq: GSC-G302
235: 
236: 	err = caCert.Write(
237: 		filepath.Join(o.ToolsCert.Path, o.ToolsCert.CANamePrefix+clabtypes.CertFileSuffix),
238: 		filepath.Join(o.ToolsCert.Path, o.ToolsCert.CANamePrefix+clabtypes.KeyFileSuffix),
239: 		"",
240: 	)
241: 	if err != nil {
242: 		return err
243: 	}
244: 
245: 	return nil
246: }
247: 
248: // signCert creates node certificate and sign it with CA.
249: func signCert(o *Options) error {
250: 	var err error
251: 
252: 	if o.ToolsCert.Path == "" {
253: 		o.ToolsCert.Path, err = os.Getwd()
254: 		if err != nil {
255: 			return err
256: 		}
257: 	}
258: 
259: 	ca := clabcert.NewCA()
260: 
261: 	var caCert *clabcert.Certificate
262: 
263: 	log.Debugf("CA cert path: %q", o.ToolsCert.CACertPath)
264: 
265: 	if o.ToolsCert.CACertPath != "" {
266: 		// we might also honor the External CA env vars here
267: 		caCert, err = clabcert.NewCertificateFromFile(
268: 			o.ToolsCert.CACertPath,
269: 			o.ToolsCert.CAKeyPath,
270: 			"",
271: 		)
272: 		if err != nil {
273: 			return err
274: 		}
275: 	}
276: 
277: 	// set loaded certificate to a CA and initialize a signer
278: 	err = ca.SetCACert(caCert)
279: 	if err != nil {
280: 		return err
281: 	}
282: 
283: 	log.Info("Creating and signing certificate",
284: 		"Hosts", o.ToolsCert.CertHosts,
285: 		"CN", o.ToolsCert.CommonName,
286: 		"C", o.ToolsCert.Country,
287: 		"L", o.ToolsCert.Locality,
288: 		"O", o.ToolsCert.Organization,
289: 		"OU", o.ToolsCert.OrganizationUnit,
290: 	)
291: 
292: 	expDuration, err := time.ParseDuration(o.ToolsCert.Expiry)
293: 	if err != nil {
294: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed parsing expiry %s", o.ToolsCert.Expiry)
295: 	}
296: 
297: 	nodeCert, err := ca.GenerateAndSignNodeCert(
298: 		&clabcert.NodeCSRInput{
299: 			Hosts:            o.ToolsCert.CertHosts,
300: 			CommonName:       o.ToolsCert.CommonName,
301: 			Country:          o.ToolsCert.Country,
302: 			Locality:         o.ToolsCert.Locality,
303: 			Organization:     o.ToolsCert.Organization,
304: 			OrganizationUnit: o.ToolsCert.OrganizationUnit,
305: 			Expiry:           expDuration,
306: 			KeySize:          int(o.ToolsCert.KeySize),
307: 		})
308: 	if err != nil {
309: 		return err
310: 	}
311: 
312: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(
313: 		o.ToolsCert.Path,
314: 		clabconstants.PermissionsOpen,
315: 	) // skipcq: GSC-G302
316: 
317: 	err = nodeCert.Write(
318: 		filepath.Join(o.ToolsCert.Path, o.ToolsCert.CertNamePrefix+clabtypes.CertFileSuffix),
319: 		filepath.Join(o.ToolsCert.Path, o.ToolsCert.CertNamePrefix+clabtypes.KeyFileSuffix),
320: 		filepath.Join(o.ToolsCert.Path, o.ToolsCert.CertNamePrefix+clabtypes.CSRFileSuffix))
321: 	if err != nil {
322: 		return err
323: 	}
324: 
325: 	return nil
326: }

35: func versionCmd(o *Options) (*cobra.Command, error) {
36: 	c := &cobra.Command{
37: 		Use:   "version",
38: 		Short: "Show containerlab version or upgrade",
39: 		RunE: func(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
40: 			return printVersionInfoFn(cobraCmd, o)
41: 		},
42: 	}
43: 
44: 	c.Flags().BoolVarP(&o.Version.Short, "short", "s", false, "Print just the version number")
45: 	c.Flags().BoolVarP(&o.Version.JSON, "json", "j", false, "Print version info as json")
46: 
47: 	c.AddCommand(
48: 		&cobra.Command{
49: 			Use:   "check",
50: 			Short: "Check if a new version of containerlab is available",
51: 			RunE: func(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
52: 				// We'll use a short 5-second timeout for the remote request
53: 				ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(cobraCmd.Context(), versionCheckTimeout)
54: 				defer cancel()
55: 
56: 				m := getVersionManager()
57: 				m.DisplayNewVersionAvailable(ctx, true)
58: 
59: 				return nil
60: 			},
61: 		},

65: 			Aliases: []string{"update"},
66: 			PreRunE: func(_ *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
67: 				return clabutils.CheckAndGetRootPrivs()
68: 			},
69: 			RunE: upgrade,
70: 		},

83: // docsLinkFromVer: creates a documentation path for a given version
84: // e.g., for 0.15.0 => 0.15/
85: //
86: // for 0.15.1 => 0.15/#0151.
87: func docsLinkFromVer(ver string) string {
88: 	v, err := gover.NewVersion(ver)
89: 	if err != nil {
90: 		return "" // fallback
91: 	}
92: 
93: 	segments := v.Segments()
94: 	major := segments[0]
95: 	minor := segments[1]
96: 	patch := segments[2]
97: 
98: 	relSlug := fmt.Sprintf("%d.%d/", major, minor)
99: 	if patch != 0 {
100: 		relSlug += fmt.Sprintf("#%d%d%d", major, minor, patch)
101: 	}
102: 
103: 	return relSlug
104: }
105: 
106: func printVersionInfoFn(_ *cobra.Command, o *Options) error {
107: 	versionData := struct {
108: 		Version      string `json:"version"`
109: 		Commit       string `json:"commit"`
110: 		Date         string `json:"date"`
111: 		RepoUrl      string `json:"repository"`
112: 		ReleaseNotes string `json:"releaseNotes"`
113: 	}{
114: 		Version:      Version,
115: 		Commit:       commit,
116: 		Date:         date,
117: 		RepoUrl:      repoUrl,
118: 		ReleaseNotes: fmt.Sprintf("https://containerlab.dev/rn/%s", docsLinkFromVer(Version)),
119: 	}
120: 
121: 	if o.Version.Short {
122: 		fmt.Println(Version)
123: 		return nil
124: 	}
125: 
126: 	if o.Version.JSON {
127: 		j, err := json.Marshal(versionData)
128: 		if err != nil {
129: 			return err
130: 		}
131: 
132: 		fmt.Println(string(j))
133: 
134: 		return nil
135: 	}
136: 
137: 	fmt.Println(projASCIILogo)
138: 	fmt.Printf("    version: %s\n", Version)
139: 	fmt.Printf("     commit: %s\n", commit)
140: 	fmt.Printf("       date: %s\n", date)
141: 	fmt.Printf("     source: %s\n", repoUrl)
142: 	fmt.Printf(" rel. notes: %s\n", versionData.ReleaseNotes)
143: 
144: 	return nil
145: }
146: 
147: // printNewVersionInfo prints instructions about a
148: // newer version, so we don't duplicate that string in multiple places.
149: func printNewVersionInfo(ver string) {
150: 	relSlug := docsLinkFromVer(ver)
151: 	log.Info(
152: 		"containerlab version", "🎉", fmt.Sprintf(
153: 			"A newer containerlab version (%s) is available!\nRelease notes: https://containerlab.dev/rn/%s\nRun 'clab version upgrade' or see https://containerlab.dev/install/ for other installation options.",
154: 			ver,
155: 			relSlug,
156: 		),
157: 	)
158: }
159: 
160: func upgrade(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
161: 	f, err := os.CreateTemp("", "containerlab")
162: 	if err != nil {
163: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create temp file: %w", err)
164: 	}
165: 
166: 	defer os.Remove(f.Name())
167: 
168: 	err = clabutils.CopyFileContents(cobraCmd.Context(), downloadURL, f)
169: 	if err != nil {
170: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to download upgrade script: %w", err)
171: 	}
172: 
173: 	c := exec.CommandContext(cobraCmd.Context(), "sudo", "-E", "bash", f.Name())
174: 	// pass the environment variables to the upgrade script
175: 	// so that GITHUB_TOKEN is available
176: 	c.Env = os.Environ()
177: 
178: 	c.Stdout = os.Stdout
179: 	c.Stderr = os.Stderr
180: 
181: 	err = c.Run()
182: 	if err != nil {
183: 		return fmt.Errorf("upgrade failed: %w", err)
184: 	}
185: 
186: 	return nil
187: }

Reasoning
Now important: `version upgrade` has its own `PreRunE` calling `CheckAndGetRootPrivs` — and it's NOT in the runtime-independent list. Good. But note the interplay: for `version upgrade` with podman, root check happens in preRunFn too (not runtime-independent). Fine.

Wait — one subtle thing: `version upgrade`'s PreRunE calls `CheckAndGetRootPrivs()`. For runtime-independent commands, root check is skipped. `version upgrade` is not in the map → root check happens in preRunFn. OK.

But there's a subtlety with `DropRootPrivs` and `CheckAndGetRootPrivs`. Let me look at utils functions.

15: 	ROOT_UID              = 0
16: 	NOMODIFY              = -1
17: )
18: 
19: func CheckAndGetRootPrivs() error {
20: 	_, euid, suid := unix.Getresuid()
21: 	if euid != 0 && suid != 0 {
22: 		return fmt.Errorf(
23: 			"this containerlab command requires root privileges or root via SUID to run, effective UID: %v SUID: %v",
24: 			euid,
25: 			suid,
26: 		)
27: 	}
28: 
29: 	// If we are not running directly as root, and SUID is properly set, attempt to get root
30: 	// privileges
31: 	if euid != 0 && suid == 0 {
32: 		clabGroupExists, err := UnixGroupExists(CLAB_AUTHORIZED_GROUP)
33: 		if err != nil {
34: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed to lookup containerlab admin group: %w", err)
35: 		}
36: 
37: 		if clabGroupExists {
38: 			currentEffUser, err := user.Current()
39: 			if err != nil {
40: 				return fmt.Errorf("failed to retrieve current user details: %w", err)
41: 			}
42: 
43: 			userInClabGroup, err := UserInUnixGroup(currentEffUser.Username, CLAB_AUTHORIZED_GROUP)
44: 			if err != nil {
45: 				return fmt.Errorf("failed to check containerlab admin group membership: %w", err)
46: 			}
47: 
48: 			if !userInClabGroup {
49: 				return fmt.Errorf(
50: 					"user '%v' is not part of containerlab admin group 'clab_admins', which is required to execute this command.\nTo add yourself to this group, run the following command:\n\t$ sudo usermod -aG clab_admins %v",
51: 					currentEffUser.Username,
52: 					currentEffUser.Username,
53: 				)
54: 			}
55: 
56: 			log.Debug("Group membership check passed")
57: 		} else {
58: 			log.Debug(
59: 				"Containerlab admin group 'clab_admins' does not exist, skipping group membership check",
60: 			)
61: 		}
62: 
63: 		err = obtainRootPrivs()
64: 		if err != nil {
65: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed to obtain root privileges: %w", err)
66: 		}
67: 	} else if euid == 0 {
68: 		log.Debugf("Already running as root, skipping root privilege escalation")
69: 	}
70: 
71: 	return nil
72: }
73: 
74: func obtainRootPrivs() error {
75: 	// Escalate to root privileges, changing saved UIDs to root/current group to be able to retain
76: 	// privilege escalation
77: 	err := changePrivileges(0, os.Getgid(), 0, os.Getgid())
78: 	if err != nil {
79: 		return err
80: 	}
81: 
82: 	log.Debug("Obtained root privileges")
83: 
84: 	return nil
85: }
86: 
87: func DropRootPrivs() error {
88: 	// Drop privileges to the running user, retaining current saved IDs
89: 	err := changePrivileges(os.Getuid(), os.Getgid(), -1, -1)
90: 	if err != nil {
91: 		return err
92: 	}
93: 
94: 	log.Debug("Dropped root privileges")
95: 
96: 	return nil
97: }
98: 
99: func changePrivileges(new_uid, new_gid, saved_uid, saved_gid int) error {
100: 	if err := unix.Setresuid(-1, new_uid, saved_uid); err != nil {
101: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to set UID: %w", err)
102: 	}
103: 	if err := unix.Setresgid(-1, new_gid, saved_gid); err != nil {
104: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to set GID: %w", err)
105: 	}
106: 	log.Debugf("Changed running UIDs to UID: %d GID: %d", new_uid, new_gid)
107: 	return nil
108: }

324: func TestRuntimeIndependentCommandsExist(t *testing.T) {
325: 	optionsInstance = nil
326: 	t.Cleanup(func() { optionsInstance = nil })
327: 
328: 	root, err := Entrypoint()
329: 	if err != nil {
330: 		t.Fatal(err)
331: 	}
332: 	root.InitDefaultHelpCmd()
333: 
334: 	for commandPath := range runtimeIndependentCommands {
335: 		// Cobra adds the completion request commands only when it executes
336: 		if strings.HasPrefix(commandPath, "__") {
337: 			continue
338: 		}
339: 
340: 		found, _, err := root.Find(strings.Split(commandPath, "."))
341: 		if err != nil || getCommandPath(found) != commandPath {

343: 		}

A. `getTopoFilePath` ordering issue: In preRunFn, `getTopoFilePath` runs AFTER the root check. For runtime-independent commands, the process stays non-root. Actually more interesting: `DropRootPrivs` then root check — for commands needing root, privileges get re-obtained via SUID model. That's existing behavior.

298: 		options = append(options, clabcore.WithTopologyVarsFiles(o.VarsFiles))

300:

Reasoning
Key observation from options.go lines 305-308:

```go
if o.TopologyFile == "" && o.TopologyName != "" &&
    !clablabruntime.IsLabRuntimeName(o.Runtime) {
    options = append(options, clabcore.WithTopologyFromLab(o.TopologyName, o.VarsFiles))
}
```

So when a topology is given by name alone (no topo file), containerlab reconstructs the topology from the running lab's containers (`WithTopologyFromLab`) — which requires the runtime. This confirms `topologyFromFile` semantics: with `--name` alone the topology is read from containers. So `validate --name lab` needs the runtime → correctly NOT runtime-independent. And `graph --dot --name lab -t file` — topo file wins. Good, matches.

Wait — actually there's a subtlety: with BOTH `--name` and `-t`, `WithTopologyFromLab` is NOT added (TopologyFile != ""). So reading from file. Good.

But hold on: `topologyFromFile` returns true when TopologyName == "" even if TopologyFile == "" — meaning the topo file will be auto-discovered from cwd by getTopoFilePath. That's still from-file. Good.

Now let me check `SetUIDAndGID` in file.go, and also check the `__complete` command path: `getCommandPath` for the `__complete` command — Name() = "__complete". So path "__complete" — matches map key `cobra.ShellCompRequestCmd` = "__complete". Good.

Now, an important potential issue: `getTopoFilePath` runs for `graph` and `validate` and may invoke `processGitTopoFile` when the topology file is a git URL and not available locally. Let me check SetUIDAndGID behavior when running as non-root:

Now let me consider the remaining subtle issues:

1. **`graph --dot` with git-URL topology and podman**: `processGitTopoFile` runs as non-root now. `SetUIDAndGID` → `GetRealUserIDs` — check: if the process was never root (e.g., plain user running podman build), GetRealUserIDs likely returns SUDO_UID env or current uid. recursiveChown chowns to own uid/gid — as a plain non-root user, chown to own uid/gid is allowed (POSIX: unprivileged user may chown to own uid... actually chown by unprivileged users is restricted on many systems — Linux allows chown to the same uid/gid? Linux: unprivileged chown is allowed only to change group to a group the user belongs to and uid must be unchanged... Actually Linux disallows unprivileged chown to change owner uid at all; but if uid is unchanged and gid is one of user's groups, it's allowed. os.Chown(name, ownUID, ownGID) as the file owner — changing uid to itself is fine, gid to its own primary group — allowed. So SetUIDAndGID likely succeeds. And on failure it's logged as error and continues anyway. So not a defect.

2. **GetRealUserIDs when not under sudo**: let me quickly check. If it errors (e.g., SUDO_UID unset), SetUIDAndGID returns err → processGitTopoFile logs error and continues. Fine.

3. **`DropRootPrivs` before check**: existing.

37:   $ containerlab completion zsh > "${fpath[1]}/_containerlab"

40:

42:

44:

46:   $ containerlab completion fish > ~/.config/fish/completions/containerlab.fish
