<content>
1: package cmd
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"context"
5: 	"fmt"
6: 	"net/http"
7: 	"os"
8: 	"strings"
9: 	"sync"
10: 	"time"
11: 
12: 	"github.com/charmbracelet/log"
13: 	gover "github.com/hashicorp/go-version"
14: )
15: 
16: const (
17: 	versionCheckInterval            = 100 * time.Millisecond
18: 	expectedClabReleaseVersionParts = 2
19: )
20: 
21: var (
22: 	versionManagerInstance     Manager   //nolint:gochecknoglobals
23: 	versionManagerInstanceOnce sync.Once //nolint:gochecknoglobals
24: )
25: 
26: // InitManager initializes the version manager, we have an explicit init so we can capture the root
27: // context to ensure appropriate cancellation during the version fetching if necessary.
28: func initVersionManager(
29: 	ctx context.Context,
30: ) {
31: 	versionManagerInstanceOnce.Do(func() {
32: 		m := &manager{
33: 			verLock:        sync.Mutex{},
34: 			currentVersion: mustParseVersion(Version),
35: 		}
36: 
37: 		versionCheckStatus := os.Getenv("CLAB_VERSION_CHECK")
38: 
39: 		log.Debugf("Env: CLAB_VERSION_CHECK=%s", versionCheckStatus)
40: 
41: 		if strings.Contains(strings.ToLower(versionCheckStatus), "disable") {
42: 			m.fetchDisabled = true
43: 		}
44: 
45: 		// run it so we check the version stuff in the background while user stuff is happening
46: 		go m.run(ctx)
47: 
48: 		versionManagerInstance = m
49: 	})
50: }
51: 
52: func getVersionManager() Manager {
53: 	if versionManagerInstance == nil {
54: 		// be defensive, should be initialized during first cmd spin up though
55: 		panic(
56: 			"version manager instance is nil, this should not happen",
57: 		)
58: 	}
59: 
60: 	return versionManagerInstance
61: }
62: 
63: // Manager is the config manager interface defining the version manager methods.
64: type Manager interface {
65: 	GetLatestVersion(ctx context.Context) *gover.Version
66: 	DisplayNewVersionAvailable(ctx context.Context, printCurrentVersion bool)
67: }
68: 
69: type manager struct {
70: 	fetchDisabled  bool
71: 	verLock        sync.Mutex
72: 	currentVersion *gover.Version
73: 	latestVersion  *gover.Version
74: }
75: 
76: func (m *manager) GetLatestVersion(ctx context.Context) *gover.Version {
77: 	if m.fetchDisabled {
78: 		return nil
79: 	}
80: 
81: 	t := time.NewTicker(versionCheckInterval)
82: 	defer t.Stop()
83: 
84: 	for {
85: 		select {
86: 		case <-ctx.Done():
87: 			return nil
88: 		case <-t.C:
89: 			m.verLock.Lock()
90: 
91: 			latestVer := m.latestVersion
92: 
93: 			// just locking so we can safely write it in, then reads are fine
94: 			m.verLock.Unlock()
95: 
96: 			if latestVer != nil {
97: 				return latestVer
98: 			}
99: 		}
100: 	}
101: }
102: 
103: // DisplayNewVersionAvailable checks and displays if a new version is available.
104: // If printCurrentVersion is true, it will also print a message if the user is on the latest
105: // version,
106: // which is used in the "version check" command.
107: func (m *manager) DisplayNewVersionAvailable(ctx context.Context, printCurrentVersion bool) {
108: 	latestVersion := m.GetLatestVersion(ctx)
109: 
110: 	switch {
111: 	case latestVersion == nil:
112: 		log.Info("Failed fetching latest version information")
113: 	case latestVersion.GreaterThan(m.currentVersion):
114: 		printNewVersionInfo(latestVersion.String())
115: 	default:
116: 		if printCurrentVersion {
117: 			log.Infof("You are on the latest version (%s)", Version)
118: 		}
119: 	}
120: }
121: 
122: func (m *manager) run(ctx context.Context) {
123: 	if m.fetchDisabled {
124: 		return
125: 	}
126: 
127: 	client := &http.Client{
128: 		// Don’t follow redirects
129: 		CheckRedirect: func(_ *http.Request, _ []*http.Request) error {
130: 			return http.ErrUseLastResponse
131: 		},
132: 	}
133: 
134: 	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(
135: 		ctx, http.MethodHead,
136: 		fmt.Sprintf("%s/releases/latest", repoUrl),
137: 		http.NoBody,
138: 	)
139: 	if err != nil {
140: 		log.Debugf("error occurred during latest version fetch: %v", err)
141: 
142: 		return
143: 	}
144: 
145: 	resp, err := client.Do(req)
146: 	if err != nil {
147: 		log.Debugf("error occurred during latest version fetch: %v", err)
148: 
149: 		return
150: 	}
151: 
152: 	if resp == nil {
153: 		log.Debug("no payload received during latest version fetch")
154: 
155: 		return
156: 	}
157: 
158: 	defer func() {
159: 		_ = resp.Body.Close()
160: 	}()
161: 
162: 	locationHeader := resp.Header.Get("Location")
163: 
164: 	releaseParts := strings.Split(locationHeader, "releases/tag/")
165: 
166: 	if len(releaseParts) != expectedClabReleaseVersionParts {
167: 		log.Debugf("could not properly parse release version from %q", locationHeader)
168: 
169: 		return
170: 	}
171: 
172: 	m.verLock.Lock()
173: 	defer m.verLock.Unlock()
174: 
175: 	m.latestVersion = mustParseVersion(releaseParts[1])
176: }
177: 
178: func mustParseVersion(v string) *gover.Version {
179: 	parsed, err := gover.NewVersion(v)
180: 	if err != nil {
181: 		// swallow parsing errors and just log it
182: 		log.Debugf("error occurred parsing version string %q, err: %v", v, err)
183: 
184: 		return nil
185: 	}
186: 
187: 	return parsed
188: }

<content>
478: ```{.bash .no-select .no-copy}
479: ... clab_admins ...
480: #   ^ user is member of clab_admins group
481: ```
482: </div>
483: 
484: ///
485: 
486: Additionally, to prevent unauthorized users from gaining root-level privileges through Containerlab, the usage of privileged Containerlab commands is gated behind a Unix user group membership check. Privileged Containerlab commands can only be performed by users who are part of the `clab_admins` group.  
487: By default (starting with version `0.63.0`), the `clab_admins` Unix group is created during the initial installation of Containerlab, and the user installing Containerlab is automatically added to this user group. Additional users who require access to privileged Containerlab commands should also be added to this user group.
488: 
489: Users who are _not_ part of this group can still execute non-privileged commands, such as:
490: 
491: * completion
492: * exec (requires `docker` group membership)
493: * generate
494: * graph
495: * inspect (requires `docker` group membership)
496: * save
497: * tools cert
498: * validate
499: * version (no upgrade)
500: 
501: Non-privileged commands are executed as the user running the Containerlab commands. Privileged commands are executed as root during runtime.  
502: All of these run without root privileges only with the default container runtime, Docker. With other runtimes, such as Podman, only the commands that don't use the container runtime do:
503: 
504: * completion
505: * generate (no `--deploy`)
506: * graph with `--dot`, `--mermaid` or `--offline` (no `--drawio`, not with `--name` alone)
507: * help
508: * tools cert
509: * tui with `--demo`
510: * validate (not with `--name` alone)
511: * version (no upgrade)
512: 
513: To allow _any user on the host_ to use all Containerlab commands, simply delete the `clab_admins` Unix group.
514: 
515: /// danger
516: Much like the `docker` group, any users part of the `clab_admins` group are effectively given root-level privileges to the system running Containerlab.
517: 
518: **If this group does not exist and the binary still has the SUID bit set, any user who can run Containerlab should be treated as having root privileges.**
519: ///
520: 
521: To **disable sudo-less operation**, simply unset the SUID flag on the Containerlab binary:
522: 
523: ```
524: sudo chmod u-s `which containerlab`
525: ```
526: 
527: Containerlab installers will **not** attempt to set the SUID flag or create the `clab_admins` group as long as the empty file `/etc/containerlab/suid_setup_done` exists.  
528: This file is automatically created during the first installation of Containerlab `0.63.0` or newer.
529: 
530: [^1]: Most containerized NOS will require >1 vCPU. RAM size depends on the lab size. IPv6 should not be disabled in the kernel.
